Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Yeah, um… I guess what made me finally bring it up is my eating has been kind of off the past few weeks. Like, not in a dramatic way exactly, but enough that I’ve noticed it. I’ll realize I skipped breakfast and lunch without really meaning to—like the day just goes by and I haven’t eaten—and then at dinner I’m just sort of picking at the food, even when my wife cooked something I normally like.

And it’s not that I’m trying to lose weight or anything. I just don’t seem to have much appetite. My jeans are starting to feel loose, too, which is kind of what made me think, okay, maybe I should actually mention this to someone instead of just brushing it off.

I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve completely stopped caring about things or anything like that. It’s more like… I haven’t really been paying close attention to it, I guess. If you asked my wife she might have a better answer than me, because she notices that kind of thing more.

I still like the idea of doing the things I usually like, if that makes sense. I don’t feel like I suddenly hate being around people or don’t want to see my family. But I guess some days I’m just not as… engaged? It’s hard to tell whether that’s interest or just me being distracted or tired or whatever. So I wouldn’t say there’s some obvious thing where I used to love a hobby and now I don’t care at all. I haven’t really noticed that specifically.

Yeah… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m crying all day or anything like that, but most days lately I wake up and there’s just this kind of heavy, flat feeling there already. Like before anything has even happened, the day feels sort of muted.

I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I mean, I don’t feel like I’ve been out running errands all day or doing anything that would obviously make me tired, but I also haven’t really been tracking my energy closely, if that makes sense.

Some days I do feel kind of slowed down, but I don’t know if I’d call it “tired” exactly. It’s more like I’m just not getting much momentum going. But then I’ll think, well, maybe that’s just because I’m sitting around more or not eating normally, or because the day feels kind of heavy in general. So I don’t want to say yes like it’s this clear thing where I’m exhausted all the time.

I guess I’ve noticed I’m not exactly bursting with energy, but I can’t point to it as a separate problem the way I can with the appetite stuff. It’s kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m having a hard time telling what’s what.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t really know. I mean, not in a clear way, I don’t think. I wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around thinking, “I’m a terrible person,” or anything that direct.

I do get kind of self-critical sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s the same thing as guilt. Like I’ll look around and think I should probably be doing more, or handling things better, or not being so… whatever this is. But it’s not like one specific thing I feel guilty about, and I don’t know that it’s been this constant thought of “I’m not good enough.”

It’s more vague than that, I guess. If I’m already feeling low, my mind can kind of drift into that territory, like maybe I’m not doing enough, maybe I’m not being very useful. But I can also step back and think, okay, that’s probably just me being hard on myself. So I’m not sure I’d count it as a major thing.

But then I’ll think, well, maybe that’s just because I’m sitting around more or not eating normally, or because the day feels kind of heavy in general. So I don’t want to say yes like it’s this clear thing where I’m exhausted all the time.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not exactly bursting with energy, but I can’t point to it as a separate problem the way I can with the appetite stuff. It’s kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m having a hard time telling what’s what.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or thinking you’re not good enough, even if you know it doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, when you say “consistently,” I don’t want to make it sound like I’m in the exact same mood every single minute, because there are moments where I’m distracted or I can talk normally or whatever. It’s like I wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, flat feeling already sitting there. I’ll try to do something that usually helps me feel more like myself, like picking up my guitar, and I can still physically do it, but it doesn’t really shift anything. It’s more like I’m playing at feeling normal than actually feeling better.

So yeah, over the past couple weeks, I’d say most days have had that low, muted feeling for most of the day. Not always intense, but pretty regularly there.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I think so. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.

Like, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. It’s not that I don’t understand it at all, it’s more like it won’t “click” into the next step. I’ll stare at it and think, okay, what was I about to change here? And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.

It happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.

So yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.

S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or thinking you’re not good enough, even if you know it doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, not in a clear way, I don’t think. I wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around thinking, “I’m a terrible person,” or anything that direct.\n\nI do get kind of self-critical sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s the same thing as guilt. And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. I don’t really feel like I’m bouncing my leg or pacing around or like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.

Like I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.

It’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.

I wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around thinking, “I’m a terrible person,” or anything that direct.\n\nI do get kind of self-critical sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s the same thing as guilt. Like I’ll look around and think I should probably be doing more, or handling things better, or not being so… whatever this is. But it’s not like one specific thing I feel guilty about, and I don’t know that it’s been this constant thought of “I’m not good enough.”\n\nIt’s more vague than that, I guess. If I’m already feeling low, my mind can kind of drift into that territory, like maybe I’m not doing enough, maybe I’m not being very useful. But I can also step back and think, okay, that’s probably just me being hard on myself. So I’m not sure I’d count it as a major thing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, when you say “consistently,” I don’t want to make it sound like I’m in the exact same mood every single minute, because there are moments where I’m distracted or I can talk normally or whatever. But overall, most days lately, it’s there for a lot of the day.\n\nIt’s like I wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, flat feeling already sitting there. And then the apartment feels really quiet in this kind of empty way, not a calm way. I’ll try to do something that usually helps me feel more like myself, like picking up my guitar, and I can still physically do it, but it doesn’t really shift anything. It’s more like I’m playing at feeling normal than actually feeling better.\n\nSo yeah, over the past couple weeks, I’d say most days have had that low, muted feeling for most of the day. Not always intense, but pretty regularly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.\n\nLike, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. It’s not that I don’t understand it at all, it’s more like it won’t “click” into the next step. I’ll stare at it and think, okay, what was I about to change here? And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. I don’t really feel like I’m bouncing my leg or pacing around or like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.\n\nLike I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.\n\nIt’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve “lost interest” exactly. I still think about doing the things I normally like, and part of me still wants to do them, if that makes sense.

It’s more that I don’t always know what I’m getting out of them right now. Like with stuff I usually enjoy, I might start it and then kind of question whether I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s what I normally do. But I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped caring about those things or that nothing can feel good at all.

So… maybe less clear pleasure sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d call it a full loss of interest. I still have the impulse to go toward those things. It just feels a little harder to tell if they’re landing the way they usually do.

I still think about doing the things I normally like, and part of me still wants to do them, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more that I don’t always know what I’m getting out of them right now. I’ll realize I skipped breakfast and lunch without really meaning to—like the day just goes by and I haven’t eaten—and then at dinner I’m just sort of picking at the food, even when my wife cooked something I normally like.\n\nAnd it’s not that I’m trying to lose weight or anything. I just don’t seem to have much appetite. My jeans are starting to feel loose, too, which is kind of what made me think, okay, maybe I should actually mention this to someone instead of just brushing it off.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve completely stopped caring about things or anything like that. It’s more like… I haven’t really been paying close attention to it, I guess. If you asked my wife she might have a better answer than me, because she notices that kind of thing more.\n\nI still like the idea of doing the things I usually like, if that makes sense. I don’t feel like I suddenly hate being around people or don’t want to see my family. But I guess some days I’m just not as… engaged? It’s hard to tell whether that’s interest or just me being distracted or tired or whatever. So I wouldn’t say there’s some obvious thing where I used to love a hobby and now I don’t care at all. I haven’t really noticed that specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, nearly every day, even if it's not intense or debilitating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m crying all day or anything like that, but most days lately I wake up and there’s just this kind of heavy, flat feeling there already. Like before anything has even happened, the day feels sort of muted.\n\nIt’s especially noticeable in the apartment in the morning. It just feels too quiet, if that makes sense. Not peaceful quiet, more like… empty quiet. And I’ll think, okay, maybe I’ll pick up my guitar for a bit, because that usually helps me feel more like myself, but even that doesn’t really lift it. I’ll sit there with it and kind of go through the motions, and the feeling is still there underneath.\n\nSo yeah, I guess “down” is probably the right word. Not dramatic, not like I can’t function at all, but it’s been there most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even if you haven’t been doing anything physically taxing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I mean, I don’t feel like I’ve been out running errands all day or doing anything that would obviously make me tired, but I also haven’t really been tracking my energy closely, if that makes sense.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of slowed down, but I don’t know if I’d call it “tired” exactly. It’s more like I’m just not getting much momentum going. But then I’ll think, well, maybe that’s just because I’m sitting around more or not eating normally, or because the day feels kind of heavy in general. So I don’t want to say yes like it’s this clear thing where I’m exhausted all the time.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not exactly bursting with energy, but I can’t point to it as a separate problem the way I can with the appetite stuff. It’s kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m having a hard time telling what’s what.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or thinking you’re not good enough, even if you know it doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, not in a clear way, I don’t think. I wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around thinking, “I’m a terrible person,” or anything that direct.\n\nI do get kind of self-critical sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s the same thing as guilt. Like I’ll look around and think I should probably be doing more, or handling things better, or not being so… whatever this is. But it’s not like one specific thing I feel guilty about, and I don’t know that it’s been this constant thought of “I’m not good enough.”\n\nIt’s more vague than that, I guess. If I’m already feeling low, my mind can kind of drift into that territory, like maybe I’m not doing enough, maybe I’m not being very useful. But I can also step back and think, okay, that’s probably just me being hard on myself. So I’m not sure I’d count it as a major thing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, when you say “consistently,” I don’t want to make it sound like I’m in the exact same mood every single minute, because there are moments where I’m distracted or I can talk normally or whatever. But overall, most days lately, it’s there for a lot of the day.\n\nIt’s like I wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, flat feeling already sitting there. And then the apartment feels really quiet in this kind of empty way, not a calm way. I’ll try to do something that usually helps me feel more like myself, like picking up my guitar, and I can still physically do it, but it doesn’t really shift anything. It’s more like I’m playing at feeling normal than actually feeling better.\n\nSo yeah, over the past couple weeks, I’d say most days have had that low, muted feeling for most of the day. Not always intense, but pretty regularly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.\n\nLike, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. It’s not that I don’t understand it at all, it’s more like it won’t “click” into the next step. I’ll stare at it and think, okay, what was I about to change here? And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. I don’t really feel like I’m bouncing my leg or pacing around or like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.\n\nLike I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.\n\nIt’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, or found less pleasure in them than usual, even things that would normally make you happy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve “lost interest” exactly. I still think about doing the things I normally like, and part of me still wants to do them, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more that I don’t always know what I’m getting out of them right now. Like with stuff I usually enjoy, I might start it and then kind of question whether I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s what I normally do. But I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped caring about those things or that nothing can feel good at all.\n\nSo… maybe less clear pleasure sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d call it a full loss of interest. I still have the impulse to go toward those things. It just feels a little harder to tell if they’re landing the way they usually do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and the other symptoms you've described have been present for more than two weeks, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been more than two weeks. I think… about three weeks now, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife got settled into her full-time work schedule. At first I thought it was just the adjustment, like the house rhythm changed and I was sort of reacting to that, so I didn’t really mark it as “oh, this is a stretch.” But looking back, it’s been pretty consistently there since around then. Not every single minute exactly the same, but this general low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around for roughly three weeks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{
  "candidates": ["F32.1", "F32.2", "F33.1", "F33.2"],
  "note": "",
  "is_final": false
}

I don’t really feel like I’m bouncing my leg or pacing around or like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.\n\nLike I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.\n\nIt’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, or found less pleasure in them than usual, even things that would normally make you happy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve “lost interest” exactly. I still think about doing the things I normally like, and part of me still wants to do them, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more that I don’t always know what I’m getting out of them right now. I still have the impulse to go toward those things. It just feels a little harder to tell if they’re landing the way they usually do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and the other symptoms you've described have been present for more than two weeks, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been more than two weeks.   {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not two years, no. I don’t think it’s been that long.

Looking back, I’d say this sort of depressed, slowed-down pattern has been around off and on for maybe about three months. It wasn’t as obvious the whole time, and I probably wouldn’t have described it the same way at the beginning. There were days where I could kind of write it off or tell myself it was just a slump or whatever.

But the past few weeks have been the most noticeable part of it. That’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, like it wasn’t just a random bad day here and there. So… no, not two years, but not brand new either. More like a few months in the background, with the last three weeks being the part that really stood out.

I just don’t seem to have much appetite. My jeans are starting to feel loose, too, which is kind of what made me think, okay, maybe I should actually mention this to someone instead of just brushing it off.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve completely stopped caring about things or anything like that. It’s more like… I haven’t really been paying close attention to it, I guess. If you asked my wife she might have a better answer than me, because she notices that kind of thing more.\n\nI still like the idea of doing the things I usually like, if that makes sense. I don’t feel like I suddenly hate being around people or don’t want to see my family. But I guess some days I’m just not as… engaged? Not peaceful quiet, more like… empty quiet. And I’ll think, okay, maybe I’ll pick up my guitar for a bit, because that usually helps me feel more like myself, but even that doesn’t really lift it. I’ll sit there with it and kind of go through the motions, and the feeling is still there underneath.\n\nSo yeah, I guess “down” is probably the right word. Not dramatic, not like I can’t function at all, but it’s been there most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even if you haven’t been doing anything physically taxing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I mean, I don’t feel like I’ve been out running errands all day or doing anything that would obviously make me tired, but I also haven’t really been tracking my energy closely, if that makes sense.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of slowed down, but I don’t know if I’d call it “tired” exactly. It’s more like I’m just not getting much momentum going. But then I’ll think, well, maybe that’s just because I’m sitting around more or not eating normally, or because the day feels kind of heavy in general. So I don’t want to say yes like it’s this clear thing where I’m exhausted all the time.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not exactly bursting with energy, but I can’t point to it as a separate problem the way I can with the appetite stuff. It’s kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m having a hard time telling what’s what.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or thinking you’re not good enough, even if you know it doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, not in a clear way, I don’t think. I wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around thinking, “I’m a terrible person,” or anything that direct.\n\nI do get kind of self-critical sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s the same thing as guilt. Like I’ll look around and think I should probably be doing more, or handling things better, or not being so… whatever this is. But it’s not like one specific thing I feel guilty about, and I don’t know that it’s been this constant thought of “I’m not good enough.”\n\nIt’s more vague than that, I guess. If I’m already feeling low, my mind can kind of drift into that territory, like maybe I’m not doing enough, maybe I’m not being very useful. But I can also step back and think, okay, that’s probably just me being hard on myself. So I’m not sure I’d count it as a major thing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, when you say “consistently,” I don’t want to make it sound like I’m in the exact same mood every single minute, because there are moments where I’m distracted or I can talk normally or whatever. But overall, most days lately, it’s there for a lot of the day.\n\nIt’s like I wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, flat feeling already sitting there. And then the apartment feels really quiet in this kind of empty way, not a calm way. I’ll try to do something that usually helps me feel more like myself, like picking up my guitar, and I can still physically do it, but it doesn’t really shift anything. It’s more like I’m playing at feeling normal than actually feeling better.\n\nSo yeah, over the past couple weeks, I’d say most days have had that low, muted feeling for most of the day. Not always intense, but pretty regularly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.\n\nLike, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. I think so, yeah… though I’m realizing I don’t have it neatly divided into separate “episodes” in my head.

It’s hard to tell whether that’s interest or just me being distracted or tired or whatever. So I wouldn’t say there’s some obvious thing where I used to love a hobby and now I don’t care at all. I haven’t really noticed that specifically.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, nearly every day, even if it's not intense or debilitating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m crying all day or anything like that, but most days lately I wake up and there’s just this kind of heavy, flat feeling there already. Like before anything has even happened, the day feels sort of muted.\n\nIt’s especially noticeable in the apartment in the morning. It just feels too quiet, if that makes sense. Not peaceful quiet, more like… empty quiet. And I’ll think, okay, maybe I’ll pick up my guitar for a bit, because that usually helps me feel more like myself, but even that doesn’t really lift it. I’ll sit there with it and kind of go through the motions, and the feeling is still there underneath.\n\nSo yeah, I guess “down” is probably the right word. Not dramatic, not like I can’t function at all, but it’s been there most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even if you haven’t been doing anything physically taxing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I mean, I don’t feel like I’ve been out running errands all day or doing anything that would obviously make me tired, but I also haven’t really been tracking my energy closely, if that makes sense.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of slowed down, but I don’t know if I’d call it “tired” exactly. It’s more like I’m just not getting much momentum going. But then I’ll think, well, maybe that’s just because I’m sitting around more or not eating normally, or because the day feels kind of heavy in general. So I don’t want to say yes like it’s this clear thing where I’m exhausted all the time.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not exactly bursting with energy, but I can’t point to it as a separate problem the way I can with the appetite stuff. It’s kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m having a hard time telling what’s what.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or thinking you’re not good enough, even if you know it doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, not in a clear way, I don’t think. I wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around thinking, “I’m a terrible person,” or anything that direct.\n\nI do get kind of self-critical sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s the same thing as guilt. Like I’ll look around and think I should probably be doing more, or handling things better, or not being so… whatever this is. But it’s not like one specific thing I feel guilty about, and I don’t know that it’s been this constant thought of “I’m not good enough.”\n\nIt’s more vague than that, I guess. If I’m already feeling low, my mind can kind of drift into that territory, like maybe I’m not doing enough, maybe I’m not being very useful. But I can also step back and think, okay, that’s probably just me being hard on myself. So I’m not sure I’d count it as a major thing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, when you say “consistently,” I don’t want to make it sound like I’m in the exact same mood every single minute, because there are moments where I’m distracted or I can talk normally or whatever. But overall, most days lately, it’s there for a lot of the day.\n\nIt’s like I wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, flat feeling already sitting there. And then the apartment feels really quiet in this kind of empty way, not a calm way. I’ll try to do something that usually helps me feel more like myself, like picking up my guitar, and I can still physically do it, but it doesn’t really shift anything. It’s more like I’m playing at feeling normal than actually feeling better.\n\nSo yeah, over the past couple weeks, I’d say most days have had that low, muted feeling for most of the day. Not always intense, but pretty regularly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.\n\nLike, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. It’s not that I don’t understand it at all, it’s more like it won’t “click” into the next step. I’ll stare at it and think, okay, what was I about to change here? And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. I don’t really feel like I’m bouncing my leg or pacing around or like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.\n\nLike I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.\n\nIt’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, or found less pleasure in them than usual, even things that would normally make you happy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve “lost interest” exactly. I still think about doing the things I normally like, and part of me still wants to do them, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more that I don’t always know what I’m getting out of them right now. Like with stuff I usually enjoy, I might start it and then kind of question whether I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s what I normally do. But I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped caring about those things or that nothing can feel good at all.\n\nSo… maybe less clear pleasure sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d call it a full loss of interest. I still have the impulse to go toward those things. It just feels a little harder to tell if they’re landing the way they usually do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and the other symptoms you've described have been present for more than two weeks, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been more than two weeks. I think… about three weeks now, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife got settled into her full-time work schedule. At first I thought it was just the adjustment, like the house rhythm changed and I was sort of reacting to that, so I didn’t really mark it as “oh, this is a stretch.” But looking back, it’s been pretty consistently there since around then. Not every single minute exactly the same, but this general low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around for roughly three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or struggling with low mood and related symptoms for two years or more, even if it’s been on and off during that time?\n\nPatient: Not two years, no. I don’t think it’s been that long.\n\nLooking back, I’d say this sort of depressed, slowed-down pattern has been around off and on for maybe about three months. It wasn’t as obvious the whole time, and I probably wouldn’t have described it the same way at the beginning. There were days where I could kind of write it off or tell myself it was just a slump or whatever.\n\nBut the past few weeks have been the most noticeable part of it. Yeah, I mean… not in a clean, separate way that I can point to and say, “That was definitely another episode,” if that makes sense.

But then I’ll think, well, maybe that’s just because I’m sitting around more or not eating normally, or because the day feels kind of heavy in general. So I don’t want to say yes like it’s this clear thing where I’m exhausted all the time.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not exactly bursting with energy, but I can’t point to it as a separate problem the way I can with the appetite stuff. It’s kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m having a hard time telling what’s what.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or thinking you’re not good enough, even if you know it doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, not in a clear way, I don’t think. I wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around thinking, “I’m a terrible person,” or anything that direct.\n\nI do get kind of self-critical sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s the same thing as guilt. Like I’ll look around and think I should probably be doing more, or handling things better, or not being so… whatever this is. But it’s not like one specific thing I feel guilty about, and I don’t know that it’s been this constant thought of “I’m not good enough.”\n\nIt’s more vague than that, I guess. If I’m already feeling low, my mind can kind of drift into that territory, like maybe I’m not doing enough, maybe I’m not being very useful. But I can also step back and think, okay, that’s probably just me being hard on myself. So I’m not sure I’d count it as a major thing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, when you say “consistently,” I don’t want to make it sound like I’m in the exact same mood every single minute, because there are moments where I’m distracted or I can talk normally or whatever. But overall, most days lately, it’s there for a lot of the day.\n\nIt’s like I wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, flat feeling already sitting there. And then the apartment feels really quiet in this kind of empty way, not a calm way. I’ll try to do something that usually helps me feel more like myself, like picking up my guitar, and I can still physically do it, but it doesn’t really shift anything. It’s more like I’m playing at feeling normal than actually feeling better.\n\nSo yeah, over the past couple weeks, I’d say most days have had that low, muted feeling for most of the day. Not always intense, but pretty regularly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.\n\nLike, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. It’s not that I don’t understand it at all, it’s more like it won’t “click” into the next step. I’ll stare at it and think, okay, what was I about to change here? And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. I don’t really feel like I’m bouncing my leg or pacing around or like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.\n\nLike I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.\n\nIt’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, or found less pleasure in them than usual, even things that would normally make you happy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve “lost interest” exactly. I still think about doing the things I normally like, and part of me still wants to do them, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more that I don’t always know what I’m getting out of them right now. Like with stuff I usually enjoy, I might start it and then kind of question whether I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s what I normally do. But I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped caring about those things or that nothing can feel good at all.\n\nSo… maybe less clear pleasure sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d call it a full loss of interest. I still have the impulse to go toward those things. It just feels a little harder to tell if they’re landing the way they usually do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and the other symptoms you've described have been present for more than two weeks, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been more than two weeks. I think… about three weeks now, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife got settled into her full-time work schedule. At first I thought it was just the adjustment, like the house rhythm changed and I was sort of reacting to that, so I didn’t really mark it as “oh, this is a stretch.” But looking back, it’s been pretty consistently there since around then. Not every single minute exactly the same, but this general low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around for roughly three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or struggling with low mood and related symptoms for two years or more, even if it’s been on and off during that time?\n\nPatient: Not two years, no. I don’t think it’s been that long.\n\nLooking back, I’d say this sort of depressed, slowed-down pattern has been around off and on for maybe about three months. It wasn’t as obvious the whole time, and I probably wouldn’t have described it the same way at the beginning. There were days where I could kind of write it off or tell myself it was just a slump or whatever.\n\nBut the past few weeks have been the most noticeable part of it. That’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, like it wasn’t just a random bad day here and there. So… no, not two years, but not brand new either. More like a few months in the background, with the last three weeks being the part that really stood out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time before this one where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah… though I’m realizing I don’t have it neatly divided into separate “episodes” in my head.\n\nLooking back, this depressed, slowed-down kind of pattern has been hanging around off and on for maybe about three months. It wasn’t necessarily intense the whole time, and there were stretches where I could convince myself it was just a rough patch or I was just tired or out of rhythm. But it was there enough that, in hindsight, it wasn’t just the last couple weeks appearing out of nowhere.\n\nThe past few weeks are what really made it stand out, though. That’s when it felt more consistently noticeable, like harder to brush off. So if you mean before this most noticeable stretch, then yes, I think there was already something similar going on for weeks before that… kind of in the background.\n\nDoctor: Before this most recent period of feeling down, have you ever had another time in your life lasting more than two weeks where you felt persistently low or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… not in a clean, separate way that I can point to and say, “That was definitely another episode,” if that makes sense.\n\nLooking back, what I can say is that this depressed, slowed-down kind of pattern has been hanging around off and on for about three months. It’s not like the last few weeks came out of nowhere. There were already periods before this most noticeable stretch where I felt kind of low and less engaged with things, but I think I kept explaining it away or not really counting it.\n\nThe past few weeks have just been the part where it became harder to ignore. So before this most recent period, yes, there was something similar there for more than two weeks, but it feels more like it was part of this same longer stretch over the last few months rather than a totally separate time in my life.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Before the past few months? I’m not sure I can really say yes to that.

I mean, when I look back, the thing I can actually identify is this current pattern that’s been hanging around off and on for about three months. It’s been this kind of depressed, slowed-down feeling, not necessarily constant every single day, but enough that it feels like it’s been part of the background for a while. And then the past few weeks have been the part where it became much more noticeable to me.

Before that, I don’t have a clear, distinct period I can point to that lasted more than two weeks where I’d say, “Yes, that was the same kind of low mood or loss of interest.” There might have been rough patches like anyone has, but nothing I’m confident was a separate episode like this.

Not dramatic, not like I can’t function at all, but it’s been there most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even if you haven’t been doing anything physically taxing?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I mean, I don’t feel like I’ve been out running errands all day or doing anything that would obviously make me tired, but I also haven’t really been tracking my energy closely, if that makes sense.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of slowed down, but I don’t know if I’d call it “tired” exactly. It’s more like I’m just not getting much momentum going. It’s kind of mixed in with everything else, and I’m having a hard time telling what’s what.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or thinking you’re not good enough, even if you know it doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, not in a clear way, I don’t think. I mean, when you say “consistently,” I don’t want to make it sound like I’m in the exact same mood every single minute, because there are moments where I’m distracted or I can talk normally or whatever. But overall, most days lately, it’s there for a lot of the day.\n\nIt’s like I wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, flat feeling already sitting there. And then the apartment feels really quiet in this kind of empty way, not a calm way. I’ll try to do something that usually helps me feel more like myself, like picking up my guitar, and I can still physically do it, but it doesn’t really shift anything. It’s more like I’m playing at feeling normal than actually feeling better.\n\nSo yeah, over the past couple weeks, I’d say most days have had that low, muted feeling for most of the day. Not always intense, but pretty regularly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.\n\nLike, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. It’s not that I don’t understand it at all, it’s more like it won’t “click” into the next step. I’ll stare at it and think, okay, what was I about to change here? And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. I don’t really feel like I’m bouncing my leg or pacing around or like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.\n\nLike I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.\n\nIt’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, or found less pleasure in them than usual, even things that would normally make you happy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve “lost interest” exactly. I still think about doing the things I normally like, and part of me still wants to do them, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more that I don’t always know what I’m getting out of them right now. Like with stuff I usually enjoy, I might start it and then kind of question whether I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s what I normally do. But I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped caring about those things or that nothing can feel good at all.\n\nSo… maybe less clear pleasure sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d call it a full loss of interest. I still have the impulse to go toward those things. I don’t think it’s been that long.\n\nLooking back, I’d say this sort of depressed, slowed-down pattern has been around off and on for maybe about three months. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.\n\nLike, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. It’s not that I don’t understand it at all, it’s more like it won’t “click” into the next step. I’ll stare at it and think, okay, what was I about to change here? And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. I don’t really feel like I’m bouncing my leg or pacing around or like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.\n\nLike I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.\n\nIt’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, or found less pleasure in them than usual, even things that would normally make you happy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve “lost interest” exactly. I still think about doing the things I normally like, and part of me still wants to do them, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more that I don’t always know what I’m getting out of them right now. Like with stuff I usually enjoy, I might start it and then kind of question whether I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s what I normally do. But I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped caring about those things or that nothing can feel good at all.\n\nSo… maybe less clear pleasure sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d call it a full loss of interest. I still have the impulse to go toward those things. It just feels a little harder to tell if they’re landing the way they usually do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and the other symptoms you've described have been present for more than two weeks, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been more than two weeks. I think… about three weeks now, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife got settled into her full-time work schedule. At first I thought it was just the adjustment, like the house rhythm changed and I was sort of reacting to that, so I didn’t really mark it as “oh, this is a stretch.” But looking back, it’s been pretty consistently there since around then. Not every single minute exactly the same, but this general low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around for roughly three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or struggling with low mood and related symptoms for two years or more, even if it’s been on and off during that time?\n\nPatient: Not two years, no. I don’t think it’s been that long.\n\nLooking back, I’d say this sort of depressed, slowed-down pattern has been around off and on for maybe about three months. It wasn’t as obvious the whole time, and I probably wouldn’t have described it the same way at the beginning. There were days where I could kind of write it off or tell myself it was just a slump or whatever.\n\nBut the past few weeks have been the most noticeable part of it. That’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, like it wasn’t just a random bad day here and there. So… no, not two years, but not brand new either. More like a few months in the background, with the last three weeks being the part that really stood out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time before this one where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah… though I’m realizing I don’t have it neatly divided into separate “episodes” in my head.\n\nLooking back, this depressed, slowed-down kind of pattern has been hanging around off and on for maybe about three months. Not always intense, but pretty regularly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.\n\nLike, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. It’s not that I don’t understand it at all, it’s more like it won’t “click” into the next step. I’ll stare at it and think, okay, what was I about to change here? And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. I don’t really feel like I’m bouncing my leg or pacing around or like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.\n\nLike I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.\n\nIt’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, or found less pleasure in them than usual, even things that would normally make you happy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve “lost interest” exactly. I still think about doing the things I normally like, and part of me still wants to do them, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s more that I don’t always know what I’m getting out of them right now. Like with stuff I usually enjoy, I might start it and then kind of question whether I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s what I normally do. But I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped caring about those things or that nothing can feel good at all.\n\nSo… maybe less clear pleasure sometimes, but I’m not sure I’d call it a full loss of interest. I still have the impulse to go toward those things. It just feels a little harder to tell if they’re landing the way they usually do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood and the other symptoms you've described have been present for more than two weeks, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been more than two weeks. I think… about three weeks now, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife got settled into her full-time work schedule. At first I thought it was just the adjustment, like the house rhythm changed and I was sort of reacting to that, so I didn’t really mark it as “oh, this is a stretch.” But looking back, it’s been pretty consistently there since around then. Not every single minute exactly the same, but this general low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around for roughly three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or struggling with low mood and related symptoms for two years or more, even if it’s been on and off during that time?\n\nPatient: Not two years, no. I don’t think it’s been that long.\n\nLooking back, I’d say this sort of depressed, slowed-down pattern has been around off and on for maybe about three months. It wasn’t as obvious the whole time, and I probably wouldn’t have described it the same way at the beginning. There were days where I could kind of write it off or tell myself it was just a slump or whatever.\n\nBut the past few weeks have been the most noticeable part of it. That’s when it started feeling harder to ignore, like it wasn’t just a random bad day here and there. So… no, not two years, but not brand new either. More like a few months in the background, with the last three weeks being the part that really stood out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time before this one where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah… though I’m realizing I don’t have it neatly divided into separate “episodes” in my head.\n\nLooking back, this depressed, slowed-down kind of pattern has been hanging around off and on for maybe about three months. It wasn’t necessarily intense the whole time, and there were stretches where I could convince myself it was just a rough patch or I was just tired or out of rhythm. But it was there enough that, in hindsight, it wasn’t just the last couple weeks appearing out of nowhere.\n\nThe past few weeks are what really made it stand out, though. That’s when it felt more consistently noticeable, like harder to brush off. So if you mean before this most noticeable stretch, then yes, I think there was already something similar going on for weeks before that… kind of in the background.\n\nDoctor: Before this most recent period of feeling down, have you ever had another time in your life lasting more than two weeks where you felt persistently low or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… not in a clean, separate way that I can point to and say, “That was definitely another episode,” if that makes sense.\n\nLooking back, what I can say is that this depressed, slowed-down kind of pattern has been hanging around off and on for about three months. It’s not like the last few weeks came out of nowhere. There were already periods before this most noticeable stretch where I felt kind of low and less engaged with things, but I think I kept explaining it away or not really counting it.\n\nThe past few weeks have just been the part where it became harder to ignore. So before this most recent period, yes, there was something similar there for more than two weeks, but it feels more like it was part of this same longer stretch over the last few months rather than a totally separate time in my life.\n\nDoctor: Before the past few months, have you ever had another distinct period in your life lasting more than two weeks where you felt persistently low or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Before the past few months? I’m not sure I can really say yes to that.\n\nI mean, when I look back, the thing I can actually identify is this current pattern that’s been hanging around off and on for about three months. It’s been this kind of depressed, slowed-down feeling, not necessarily constant every single day, but enough that it feels like it’s been part of the background for a while. And then the past few weeks have been the part where it became much more noticeable to me.\n\nBefore that, I don’t have a clear, distinct period I can point to that lasted more than two weeks where I’d say, “Yes, that was the same kind of low mood or loss of interest.” There might have been rough patches like anyone has, but nothing I’m confident was a separate episode like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had more than one separate time in your life before the past few months when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not more than one separate time before these past few months.\n\nI’m trying to think carefully, because I don’t want to just say no if I’m forgetting something, but… I don’t have distinct periods like that in my memory. There were definitely times I felt stressed or off or had a bad few days, maybe even a week here and there, but not something I can clearly say lasted more than two weeks and was its own separate stretch of feeling down or not interested in things.\n\nThe only thing I can really identify with any confidence is this more recent pattern over the past few months. It’s been this kind of depressed, slowed-down feeling, not necessarily constant every single day, but enough that it feels like it’s been part of the background for a while. And then the past few weeks have been the part where it became much more noticeable to me.\n\nBefore that, I don’t have a clear, distinct period I can point to that lasted more than two weeks where I’d say, “Yes, that was the same kind of low mood or loss of interest.” There might have been rough patches like anyone has, but nothing I’m confident was a separate episode like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had more than one separate time in your life before the past few months when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not more than one separate time before these past few months.\n\nI’m trying to think carefully, because I don’t want to just say no if I’m forgetting something, but… I don’t have distinct periods like that in my memory. There were definitely times I felt stressed or off or had a bad few days, maybe even a week here and there, but not something I can clearly say lasted more than two weeks and was its own separate stretch of feeling down or not interested in things.\n\nThe only thing I can really identify with any confidence is this more recent pattern over the past few months. Before that, it’s kind of blurry, but nothing stands out as more than one separate episode like what you’re asking.\n\n[Last Question]\nBefore the past three months, have you ever had two or more separate times in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks each time?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t think I can say that, no. Not two or more separate times before the past three months.

Like, if you asked whether I’ve ever had bad stretches or times where I was unhappy or kind of checked out, I’m sure I have, but I don’t remember two distinct periods before this that each lasted more than two weeks and were clearly that kind of low mood or loss of interest.

But overall, most days lately, it’s there for a lot of the day.\n\nIt’s like I wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, flat feeling already sitting there. And then the apartment feels really quiet in this kind of empty way, not a calm way. I’ll try to do something that usually helps me feel more like myself, like picking up my guitar, and I can still physically do it, but it doesn’t really shift anything. It’s more like I’m playing at feeling normal than actually feeling better.\n\nSo yeah, over the past couple weeks, I’d say most days have had that low, muted feeling for most of the day. Not always intense, but pretty regularly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. That one I can actually point to a little more clearly than the energy thing.\n\nLike, when I try to work on code, I’ll open the file and I know I’m trying to fix something or build something small, but I’ll keep rereading the same line over and over. It’s not that I don’t understand it at all, it’s more like it won’t “click” into the next step. I’ll stare at it and think, okay, what was I about to change here? And then I’ll scroll around and come back to the same spot and still not really decide what to do.\n\nIt happens with music too, maybe even more noticeably because that’s usually more intuitive for me. I’ll be arranging a song and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out whether it needs another guitar part or if the melody should move differently or if the rhythm feels off, and I just can’t make the decision. I’m not even making a bad decision — I’m just kind of stuck before the decision.\n\nSo yeah, I wouldn’t say I can’t think at all or anything dramatic like that, but there’s definitely been this foggy, stalled feeling when I try to focus on something that should be familiar to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, definitely more on the slower side than restless. It’s more like everything takes this weird amount of effort to start.\n\nLike I’ll sit down with my laptop open, and it’ll just be sitting there in front of me, ready to go, and I’ll know I need to type something or plug in the keyboard or open whatever file, and I just… don’t move for a while. Not because I’m deciding not to. It’s more like there’s a delay between the thought and my body actually doing the thing. I’ll be staring at the screen or the desk, and then eventually I’ll realize I’ve been sitting there for ages and my hands haven’t even gone to the keyboard.\n\nIt’s not dramatic like I’m frozen or anything, but compared to how I usually am, yeah, I feel kind of slowed down. Even small motions feel like they take more starting power than they should.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve lost interest in activities you used to enjoy, or found less pleasure in them than usual, even things that would normally make you happy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t know if I’d say I’ve “lost interest” exactly. At first I thought it was just the adjustment, like the house rhythm changed and I was sort of reacting to that, so I didn’t really mark it as “oh, this is a stretch.” But looking back, it’s been pretty consistently there since around then. Not every single minute exactly the same, but this general low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around for roughly three weeks.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or struggling with low mood and related symptoms for two years or more, even if it’s been on and off during that time?\n\nPatient: Not two years, no. I don’t think it’s been that long.\n\nLooking back, I’d say this sort of depressed, slowed-down pattern has been around off and on for maybe about three months.
